**Short answer: No — we have completed the core control-plane (roughly Phases 0–4, 7, 9, 10 partially, plus two unplanned repairs), but the majority of the operational/telemetry/qualification workstream (Phases 5–6, 8, 11–16) is still open.**

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

```

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52331 chars total)*

Pairs with `proxmox-cluster-infrastructure` for the node/guest layer.\n\n## Golden rule: deliver the write-functional core FIRST\n\nThe user was explicitly disappointed by a read-only monitoring shell: *\"I expected a full interface where I can actually change the models hosted by vLLM on these addresses and additionally be able to get API keys to use as backend endpoints.\"* A dashboard without writes is a failed deliverable. Build order:\n\n1. **Gateway serving real completions behind per-agent virtual keys** — issue key → run completion through it → revoke/budget-cap proven. This is the product.\n2. **Model/deployment management** — change model repo / context length / flags per endpoint; drain → apply → restart → re-verify.\n3. **Live dashboard** — probe real endpoints (vLLM `/v1/models`, DB roundtrip); never display fake or cached metrics.\n4. LDAP/SSO, budgets UI, benchmarking, power/cost after that.\n\nOnce the user authorizes a build (\"continue until finished with all stages\"), keep working the roadmap in one session where possible; deferred work (multi-node/RDMA rings, Emporia telemetry) gets explicitly SCOPED into the handoff doc — never silently dropped.\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nHermes agents / internal clients\n        |  OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://<gateway>/v1  + per-agent virtual key\n        v\nnginx (/v1 → LiteLLM :4000, /admin /api → FastAPI :8001)\n        v                          v\nLiteLLM proxy (virtual keys,     FastAPI control plane\nbudgets, load-balance, fallback)  (health probes, key mgmt UI,\n        v                          deployment control)\nPostgreSQL (litellm + app DBs on a dedicated CT/VM)\n        v\nvLLM fleet (per-node VMs, one port 8000 each; TP/DP/EP per GPU set)\n```\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- `references/litellm-deployment.md` — full LiteLLM proxy bring-up on Ubuntu 24.04 with PostgreSQL backing: dependency chain (prisma, nodejs), schema generate/push, P1010 and pg_hba fixes, systemd unit, nginx routing, key lifecycle API calls.\n- `templates/litellm_config.yaml` — known-good multi-endpoint gateway config (local vLLM only, no cloud fallback).\n- `templates/vllm-systemd.service` — vLLM unit template.\n- For node/guest/SSH layer: `proxmox-cluster-infrastructure` skill, esp. `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md` and its `scripts/ssh_run.py`.\n\n## User preferences embedded (2026-09)\n\n- **\"Fix what you create; don't leave broken artifacts\"** — when the user asks to review\n  or fix problems, they expect actual repairs + verification, not a findings report\n  (\"fix any problems that you've created\"). Prefer static IPs for infrastructure roles,\n  but if an address choice is uncertain, **fall back to DHCP rather than risk a\n  collision** (\"Use DHCP if you are confused instead of static IP assignment\") —\n  ambiguity on IP assignment is the one case where DHCP is the user-endorsed default.\n- **Write-functional core first** — monitoring-only dashboards disappoint; keys + completions + model control are the deliverable (see Golden rule).\n- **Scope, don't drop, deferred work** — the user explicitly approves working through all plan stages in one continuous run; anything genuinely deferred (e.g. 100 Gb RDMA ring: \"do this last, scope it for future work\") goes into the handoff doc as an explicit scoped section, never silently omitted.\n- **Name resources after their consumer** — LDAP groups/service accounts carry the VM/hostname (e.g. `llm-manager-vm114-admin`, `svc-llm-manager114`), not generic role names.\n- **Hashing ≠ encryption — say so when the user sees crypto tooling.** The user asked \"we don't want to encrypt any of this storage stuff... can you explain what you're doing?\" when bcrypt/argon2 appeared mid-build. Rule: when a password hash, TLS cert, or sealed-secret step shows up in output, add one plain-language line (\"this is a login check, not storage encryption; nothing on disk is encrypted; no performance impact\"). Never let crypto vocabulary pass unexplained — the user reads it as hidden storage-encryption scope creep.\n\n## Web control plane: auth + role mapping (built 2026-09-09)\n\n- **Login session flow**: FastAPI `SessionMiddleware` (key in a 600 secret file) + LDAP\n  bind-login via the service bind account, then verify the user by binding AS the user DN.\n  Role = strongest `llm-manager-vm114-*` group. Write APIs gated on role sets\n  (Operator→restarts; Admin/Security-Admin→key issuance + unit edits).\n- **nginx route table must enumerate EVERY route the app serves** — `/login`, `/logout`,\n  `/admin`, `/api/`, `/healthz` — anything unlisted falls into `location / → 302 /admin` and\n  produces an infinite redirect loop (browser: ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS; tests: 302 chains).\n  After adding app routes, `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx`.\n- **LiteLLM admin API verbs**: `/key/generate`, `/key/block`, `/key/delete`, `/key/update`\n  are POST, but **`/key/list` AND `/key/info` are GET** (POST returns 405 Method Not Allowed).\n  - `GET /key/list` returns `{\"keys\": [<raw token STRINGS>], \"total_count\": …}` — NOT\n    objects. Iterating with `k.get(...)` throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n    Fetch per-key metadata with `GET /key/info?key=<token>` (query param, not JSON body);\n    the useful fields live under `info` (`key_alias`, `spend`, `max_budget`, `blocked`,\n    `expires`, `models`).\n  - Truncated 502 responses from `/key/generate` usually mean LiteLLM can't reach its Postgres — check\n  the litellm journal for `httpx.ConnectError` before touching app code. Then diagnose the\n  unreachable-DB in order: (1) port REFUSED while ICMP/SSH work + service verified\n  listening on the DB CT = **IP collision with a physical host** (compare `ip neigh <ip>`\n  MAC vs the CT's real NIC MAC — this happened: a DB CT sat on a cluster node's corosync\n  address; see proxmox skill's network audit reference), (2) pg_hba/grants only after\n  connectivity is proven.\n- **DB relocation runbook** (moved CT across IPs): grep every consumer for the old IP\n  (`grep -rn \"10.0.20.<old>\" /opt/<app>/ /etc/<app>/`), sed-repoint app `PG_HOST` and\n  gateway `database_url` in the same pass, `systemctl restart` BOTH services, then prove\n  with the service ports (app login page + gateway `/v1/models` 401-with-bad-key = healthy\n  upstream), not just `systemctl is-active`. An unrepointed stale reference survived one\n  move and was only caught during a later network audit.\n- **Audit everything to a JSONL file** (`/var/log/llm-manager/audit.log`): login ok/denied +\n  reason, key issue/block/revoke, unit edits, restarts. The audit log is what makes\n  debugging auth failures possible (\"unknown user\" vs \"invalid credentials\" vs \"no role\"\n  each pinpoint a different broken layer).\n- **Guest control agent pattern** (no arbitrary shell): fixed-command script\n  `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh {status|get-unit|set-unit|restart|logs|nvidia}` per vLLM\n  guest; gateway host holds a dedicated SSH keypair (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/`),\n  pub key appended to each guest's `authorized_keys` via the owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec` (stdin is NOT forwarded — see proxmox skill). Unit edits: validate\n  `[Service]` + `ExecStart=` present, keep `.bak.<ts>`, `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restart —\n  the UI instructs the operator to restart separately (save ≠ apply).\n- **Keep the relay path documented for remote administration.** ssh_run.py (root+pexpect)\n  reaches Proxmox NODES only; the gateway VM takes a different path: land script on\n  owning node → `scp ubuntu@<vm-ip>` → `ssh … \"sudo bash /tmp/x.sh\"`. A session burned a\n  round-trip trying to ssh_run.py straight into the VM (`Permission denied (publickey)`).\n  Before driving a guest, check which path applies (node = root/password, VM =\n  node-relay with the baked-in key).\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- **Never invent vLLM flags.** Recover the battle-tested serve command from the target guest's root `~/.bash_history` (last working variant) and encode it in a systemd unit. Environment: `HF_HOME`, `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` are typical for this fleet.\n- **Per-deployment profiles.** The same logical model name can expose different `max_model_len` (observed 262144 vs 32768 on identical model IDs). Store concurrency/context envelopes per deployment; never assume homogeneity.\n- **Routable requires proof:** `/v1/models` returns the expected model AND a real chat completion succeeds — health endpoint alone is insufficient.\n- Qwen thinking models may emit reasoning inline unless `--reasoning-parser qwen3` is set — check parser flags when output contains \"Thinking Process...\". Thinking traces consume `max_tokens`: a \"max_tokens: 40\" test request can be fully consumed by the reasoning preamble before any answer text appears.\n- **Hybrid-Mamba models (Qwen3.x family): `--max-num-seqs` must fit the Mamba cache.** Every concurrent sequence holds one Mamba cache block; 16 GB cards at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92` fit only ~92 blocks, so vLLM's default 256 aborts during CUDA-graph capture with `RuntimeError: max_num_seqs (256) exceeds available Mamba cache blocks (92)` and the unit crash-loops. Fix: `--max-num-seqs 64` (comfortable headroom, still generous for Hermes load). Never copy the 3080 hosts' turboquant KV/attention flags onto smaller-VRAM GPUs without qualification.\n- **Crash-loop signature:** systemd reports `active` while `NRestarts` climbs, worker PIDs change between checks, and nothing listens on :8000. Don't keep polling the port — pull `journalctl -u vllm --no-pager | grep -iE \"error|RuntimeError\"` for the root cause directly. First successful boot takes ~4–5 min (weights ≈40 s/rank + torch.compile); the compile cache makes later restarts much faster.\n- **Re-registering a recovered backend into the gateway pool:** idempotent-edit `/etc/llm-manager/litellm_config.yaml` (skip if the endpoint IP is already present), `systemctl restart litellm`, then PROVE it: `GET /v1/models` with the master key AND one real chat completion through an agent virtual key (expect 200 + usage accounting).\n\n## Key issuance lifecycle\n\n- Master key + salt live in root-only secret files on the gateway host (chmod 600); never in configs, repos, shell history, or chat.\n- Issue: `POST /key/generate` `{\"key_alias\",\"models\",\"max_budget\",\"duration\"}` → raw key shown ONCE → store at `/etc/<app>/secrets/agent_key_<alias>`.\n- **Prove enforcement** with a deliberately `max_budget: 0` test key (expect `429 budget_exceeded`) before trusting any cap.\n- Local-first: config contains only local vLLM endpoints. Cloud fallback (OpenRouter) stays disabled until global spend caps exist and the key arrives via web-UI configuration, not config files.\n\n## Secret-rotation runbook (master key / DB password)\n\nTrigger: a secret value appeared in chat output, tool output, or a handoff file. Rotate\nbefore doing anything else — generate locally (`openssl rand -hex 24`), never print, stage\nas chmod-600 temp files only.\n\n- **Rotate BOTH the store and every consumer in one pass**, or services fail after\n  restart with a half-old state. Consumers for the LiteLLM stack: (a) Postgres role\n  password, (b) `pg_app_creds` / app PG_HOST-side secret file, (c) gateway\n  `database_url:` in the LiteLLM yaml, (d) gateway `master_key:` line. **Verify with\n  hashes, not by printing** (`md5sum` the file vs `hashlib.md5` of the config value —\n  a file↔config mismatch survived one rotation and caused 401s on every admin call).\n- **Peer-auth trap on Debian/Ubuntu PG containers:** `pct exec <ct> -- psql -U postgres -c\n  \"ALTER USER …\"` FAILS SILENTLY under peer auth (local socket matches OS user `root`,\n  not `postgres`) — the ALTER never applies while the command \"runs\". Use\n  `pct exec <ct> -- su postgres -c \"psql -c \\\"ALTER USER …\\\"\"` and then PROVE the change:\n  `PGPASSWORD=<new> psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U <role> -tAc 'select 1'` inside the CT. An\n  unapplied ALTER left the gateway down for an extra round-trip.\n- **Prisma-auth signature**: `P1000 Authentication failed … credentials for <user> are not\n  valid` in the litellm journal = the DB never got the new password (or config has the\n  old one). Fix the store first, then restart, then re-verify `/v1/models` + one real\n  completion before reporting done.\n- **Delivery transport**: secrets travel node→VM over the node's baked-in key (scp +\n  `install -m 600`), never through chat, history, or printed output; delete staged /tmp\n  copies on both ends after install.\n\n## Pitfalls (hard-won)\n\n- **pg_hba scoped to one DB name silently breaks later databases.** Write `host all <app-role> <cidr> scram-sha-256` when one role serves multiple databases (manager DB + litellm DB).\n- LiteLLM + prisma needs **nodejs/npm** installed for `prisma generate`; run generate with the venv's `bin` on `PATH` and explicit `--schema <venv>/litellm/proxy/schema.prisma`; first-time schema creation needs `prisma db push` (prisma migrations alone do not create tables here).\n- `P1010 denied on database X.public` = missing SCHEMA grants, not just DB grants: `GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO <role>;` inside the target DB.\n- Debian 12 LXCs have no sudo — use `runuser -u postgres -- psql`.\n- Deploy to VMs via: land script on owning node → `scp` node→guest using the node's baked-in key → `ssh <guest> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` (guest `ubuntu` user has NOPASSWD sudo).\n\n## Multi-session builds: maintain a handoff doc\n\nFor long infra builds keep `HANDOFF-<date>-<project>.md` current: state-map tables, mutation audit trail, access recipes (verified API login flows), no-touch list, ordered next steps, scoped future work, pitfalls section. The next agent (human or AI) starts cold from that one file.\n\n## Template\n\n- `templates/llm-control-agent.sh` — the guest-side fixed-command control agent\n  (status/get-unit/set-unit/restart/logs/nvidia). Deploy via owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec`; authorize the gateway host's control pubkey in each guest's\n  `authorized_keys`.", "path": "devops/self-hosted-llm-gateway/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/self-hosted-llm-gateway", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/litellm-deployment.md"], "templates": ["templates/litellm_config.yaml", "templates/llm-control-agent.sh"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

source from session secret).\n\nBatches an entire bash script into ONE ssh call (hosts rate-limit rapid reconnects),\ndrains ALL output via an expect-loop (single child.before read loses output), and\ntreats only 'Permission denied' as auth failure (qm config prints a literal\n'cipassword: ****' that false-matches password prompts).\n\nThis is the reference implementation extracted from a session-proven helper.\n\"\"\"\nimport base64\nimport os\nimport pexpect\nimport sys\n\n\ndef run(host: str, script_path: str, outfile: str, user: str = \"root\") -> str:\n    pw = os.environ.get(\"MIAM_SSH_PW\")\n    assert pw, \"MIAM_SSH_PW env var not set (do not hardcode passwords)\"\n    script = open(script_path).read()\n    b64 = base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()\n\n    child = pexpect.spawn(\n        \"/usr/bin/ssh\",\n        [\"-o\", \"StrictHostKeyChecking=no\", \"-o\", \"UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null\",\n         \"-o\", \"ConnectTimeout=10\", \"-l\", user, host,\n         \"bash\", \"-c\", f\"echo {b64} | base64 -d | bash\"],\n        encoding=\"utf-8\", timeout=60, maxread=65536)\n\n    out = []\n    while True:\n        i = child.expect([r\"[Pp]assword:\", pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT], timeout=30)\n        if i == 0:\n            child.sendline(pw)\n        elif i == 1:\n            out.append(child.before or \"\")\n            break\n        else:\n            out.append(child.before or \"\")\n\n    result = \"\".join(out)\n    if \"Permission denied\" in result:\n        result += \"\\n*** AUTH FAILED ***\"\n    with open(outfile, \"w\") as f:\n        f.write(result)\n    print(f\"wrote {len(result)} chars to {outfile}\")\n    return result", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

current_user(request):\n    return request.session.get(\"user\"), request.session.get(\"role\")\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- probes\n\ndef _load_pg_pw():\n    try:\n        with open(PG_PW_FILE) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                if line.startswith(\"PG_PW=\"):\n                    return line.strip().split(\"=\", 1)[1]\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    return None\n\n\ndef probe_vllm(host):\n    out = dict(host)\n    out[\"port\"] = 8000\n    t0 = time.time()\n    try:\n        s = requests.Session()\n        s.headers[\"Connection\"] = \"close\"\n        r = s.get(f\"http://{host['ip']}:8000/v1/models\", timeout=4)\n        latency_ms = int((time.time() - t0) * 1000)\n        if r.status_code == 200:\n            data = r.json().get(\"data\", [])\n            out.update(\n                status=\"healthy\",\n                latency_ms=latency_ms,\n                model=data[0][\"id\"] if data else None,\n                max_model_len=data[0].get(\"max_model_len\") if data else None,\n                root=data[0].get(\"root\") if data else None,\n            )\n        else:\n            out.update(status=\"http_\" + str(r.status_code), latency_ms=latency_ms)\n    except Exception as e:\n        out.update(status=\"error\", error=str(e)[:80], latency_ms=int((time.time() - t0) * 1000))\n    return out\n\n\ndef probe_pg():\n    pw = _load_pg_pw()\n    if not pw:\n        return {\"status\": \"error\", \"error\": \"no pg credentials\"}\n    t0 = time.time()\n    try:\n        c = psycopg2.connect(host=PG_HOST, dbname=PG_DB, user=PG_USER, password=pw,\n                             connect_timeout=4)\n        cur = c.cursor()\n        cur.execute(\"SELECT version()\")\n        v = cur.fetchone()[0]\n        c.close()\n        return {\"status\": \"healthy\", \"latency_ms\": int((time.time() - t0) * 1000),\n                \"version\": v.split(\",\")[0]}\n    except Exception as e:\n        return {\"status\": \"error\", \"error\": str(e)[:100]}\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- LiteLLM key API\n\ndef litellm_req(method, path, payload=None):\n    r = requests.request(method, f\"{LITELLM_URL}{path}\", json=payload,\n                         headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {LITELLM_MASTER}\"}, timeout=15)\n    try:\n        return r.status_code, r.json()\n    except Exception:\n        return r.status_code, {\"raw\": r.text[:200]}", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Full picture acquired. Key findings:

- **`llmmanager` DB is empty** — audit actually goes to JSONL only; telemetry schema never built
- **Control agent `nvidia` action works** — .161 returns live per-GPU watts (6 GPUs, 13.7–27.3W idle) → perfect foundation for power telemetry

Found another live bug: `/admin` returns `DASHBOARD_HTML` **without substituting** `__VER__/__USER__/__ROLE__` — so the UI has been showing literal placeholders and `CANEDIT` always evaluated false. Fixing the render path with my new placeholders too:

---

### [2026-09-09T22:36:48.000562+00:00] tool · tool: patch

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n@@ -749,7 +749,16 @@\n     user, role = current_user(request)\n     if not user:\n         return RedirectResponse(\"/login\", status_code=302)\n-    return HTMLResponse(DASHBOARD_HTML)\n+    # v0.4: actually render placeholders (v0.3 shipped them un-substituted — role badge,\n+    # CANEDIT/CANRESTART gating, and this session's OpenRouter panel all depend on it)\n+    can_provider = role in CAN_WRITE_KEYS\n+    html = (DASHBOARD_HTML\n+            .replace(\"__VER__\", APP_VERSION)\n+            .replace(\"__USER__\", user)\n+            .replace(\"__ROLE__\", role or \"\")\n+            .replace(\"__OR_DISABLED__\", \"\" if can_provider else \"disabled\")\n+            .replace(\"__OR_TOGGLE__\", \"Enable fallback\" if can_provider else \"Enable fallback (Admin only)\"))\n+    return HTMLResponse(html)\n \n \n @app.get(\"/\")\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"}

Critical find: **this LiteLLM version's `/key/block` takes singular `{"key": …}`, not `{"keys": […]}`** — my app passes `keys` (v0.3's format). The block endpoint silently echoed the request without blocking, which is why step 4 passed at 200. Fixing the app to match the actual gateway API:

---

### [2026-09-09T22:45:12.390581+00:00] tool · tool: patch

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n@@ -642,9 +642,17 @@\n     if not tokens:\n         audit(user, \"key_unblock\" if unblock else \"key_block\", key, \"error\", \"alias not found\")\n         return JSONResponse({\"error\": f\"no key found for alias {key!r}\"}, status_code=404)\n-    st, data = litellm_post(\"/key/unblock\" if unblock else \"/key/block\", {\"keys\": tokens})\n-    audit(user, \"key_unblock\" if unblock else \"key_block\", key, \"ok\" if st == 200 else \"error\")\n-    return {\"status\": st, \"resolved\": len(tokens), \"result\": data if st == 200 else str(data)[:150]}\n+    # this LiteLLM build's /key/block & /key/unblock take a SINGULAR {\"key\": ...};\n+    # the plural form is accepted-but-ignored (verified 2026-09-09: 401 after singular block)\n+    results = []\n+    ok = True\n+    for tok in tokens:\n+        st, data = litellm_post(\"/key/unblock\" if unblock else \"/key/block\", {\"key\": tok})\n+        results.append(st)\n+        ok = ok and st == 200\n+    audit(user, \"key_unblock\" if unblock else \"key_block\", key, \"ok\" if ok else \"error\")\n+    return {\"status\": 200 if ok else 502, \"resolved\": len(tokens),\n+            \"result\": results}\n \n \n @app.post(\"/api/keys/delete\")\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"}

### [2026-09-09T22:47:05.923119+00:00] assistant

**Root cause of the Deployments panel failure:** `run_control` passes the key *file contents* to `ssh -i` instead of the *path* — ssh interprets `-----BEGIN OPENSSH…` as a filename. This broke every control-agent call (status/unit/logs/restart). Fixing to use the path directly:

---

### [2026-09-09T22:47:06.525154+00:00] tool · tool: patch

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py\n@@ -676,8 +676,10 @@\n # ---------------------------------------------------------------- routes: deployment control\n \n def run_control(ip, action, extra_stdin=None, timeout=30):\n-    key = sec(\"keys/id_ed25519\")\n-    if not key:\n+    # v0.4 fix: pass the key PATH, not its contents (v0.3 fed the PEM text to ssh -i,\n+    # which ssh treated as a filename -> every control-agent call failed)\n+    key = os.path.join(SECRETS, \"keys/id_ed25519\")\n+    if not os.path.exists(key):\n         return 1, \"no control key configured\"\n     cmd = [\"ssh\", \"-i\", key,\n            \"-o\", \"StrictHostKeyChecking=no\", \"-o\", \"UserKnownHostsFile=/root/.ssh/known_hosts_llm\",\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/work/llm-mgr-20260909/main_v04.py"}

the other is None\n                function_call_tuples = self.tool_call_regex.findall(model_output)\n\n                # load the JSON, and then use it to build the Function and\n                # Tool Call\n                raw_function_calls = [\n                    json.loads(match[0] if match[0] else match[1])\n                    for match in function_call_tuples\n                ]\n                tool_calls = [\n                    ToolCall(\n                        type=\"function\",\n                        function=FunctionCall(\n                            name=function_call[\"name\"],\n                            # ## ✅ Delivered (all verified by live tests)

**1. Web UI controls vLLM models** — the Deployments panel was completely broken (a v0.3 bug fed the SSH key's *contents* to `ssh -i`; every control call failed). Fixed: unit viewing/editing, restarts, and logs all work now. Verified: unit write-back `UNIT_SAVED`, garbage input rejected 400, logs return 4,000 chars.

**2. API key lifecycle fully proven**

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Issue via UI | 200, key shown once |
| Completion with key | 200 |
| Block via UI | completion → **401 blocked** |
| Unblock | completion → 200 again |
| Revoke | completion → 401 |

Found & fixed: block/revoke used truncated tokens and the wrong LiteLLM payload shape (plural `keys` vs singular `key`) — blocking *silently did nothing* before. Also fixed `/admin` never rendering role placeholders (edit buttons were disabled for everyone) and a missing auth check on `/api/status`.

**3. OpenRouter fallback config** — new UI section: key field (masked, `sk-or-…` validated), daily/monthly caps, warning threshold, enable toggle. **Hard gate armed per plan §9.3:** enabling without caps+key returns 409 with the missing checklist items. *(Test caps $5/$50/$3 are saved — say the word if you want them cleared.)*

**4. Power & cost telemetry live** — new collector service samples all 24 GPUs every 20s → PG (`gpu_samples` + 1-min rollups). Dashboard shows per-host watts now/avg/peak, live rate **15.82¢/kWh** (winter = Sep-May base .09992 + riders .05830, versioned in `electricity_rates`), cost rollups 24h/7d/30d. Reading right now: .161=108W, .163=79W, .164=77W, .162=26W idle.

**5. Tool calling fixed fleet-wide** — Qwen3.6 emits *XML-style* tool calls; the `hermes` parser silently failed (raw tags in content). Rolled `--tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` + `--enable-auto-tool-choice` across all 4 hosts (staggered restarts, each verified healthy). Gateway now returns proper `tool_calls: get_weather({"city": "Marion, Iowa"})`. Streaming verified too (235 chunks).

{"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "description": "Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.", "tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "development"], "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent\ndescription: \"Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.\"\nversion: 2.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, setup, configuration, multi-agent, spawning, cli, gateway, development]\n    homepage: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n    related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode]\n---\n\n# Hermes Agent\n\nHermes Agent is an open-source AI agent framework by Nous Research that runs in your terminal, messaging platforms, and IDEs. It belongs to the same category as Claude Code (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), and OpenClaw — autonomous coding and task-execution agents that use tool calling to interact with your system. Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## WHEN TO USE\n\n- **Setting up or reconfiguring Hermes** — model, provider, gateway, tools, permissions\n- **Troubleshooting** — auth failures, tool errors, gateway connection issues, crashes\n- **Extending Hermes** — adding custom tools, plugins, skills, MCP servers, cron jobs\n- **Managing profiles** — creating, cloning, syncing, exporting/importing profiles\n- **Understanding Hermes internals** — how sessions, memory, skills, and toolsets work\n- **Contributing to Hermes** — reporting bugs, submitting PRs, running tests\n- **Configuration queries** — \"How do I set up X?\", \"Where is the config?\", \"Why is Y happening?\"\n\n## QUICK REFERENCE\n\n```bash\n# Essential commands\nhermes              # Interactive chat\nhermes setup        # Setup wizard\nhermes model        # Change model/provider\nhermes config edit  # Edit config.yaml\nhermes doctor       # Health check\nhermes tools        # Enable/disable toolsets\nhermes skills list  # List installed skills\nhermes profile list # List profiles\nhermes cron list    # List cron jobs\nhermes gateway run  # Start gateway\n```\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    Hermes Agent                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                             │\n│  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐   │\n│  │ CLI      │  │ Gateway  │  │ Cron     │  │ ACP      │   │\n│  │ (hermes) │  │ (Telegram│  │ (cron)   │  │ (IDE)    │   │\n│  │          │  │  etc.)   │  │          │  │          │   │\n│  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘   │\n│       │              │              │              │         │\n│       └──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┘         │\n│                      ▼              ▼                       │\n│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │\n│  │              Agent Core                         │       │\n│  │  • Skill Loader  • Memory Manager               │       │\n│  │  • Tool Dispatcher • Session Manager            │       │\n│  │  • Context Compressor • Credential Pool          │       │\n│  └────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘       │\n│                       ▼                                    │\n│  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐      │\n│  │ terminal │ │ file     │ │ web      │ │ browser  │      │\n│  │          │ │ search/  │ │ search/  │ │ automation│      │\n│  │          │ │ write    │ │ fetch    │ │          │      │\n│  └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘      │\n│                                                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  Profiles: ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/                       │\n│  Config: ~/.hermes/config.yaml                              │\n│  Skills: ~/.hermes/skills/ + ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/...  │\n│  Sessions: ~/.hermes/sessions/                              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## INTEGRATION POINTS\n\n| Related Skill | How They Connect |\n|---------------|-----------------|\n| **claude-code** | Claude Code runs as an external agent via Hermes terminal. Use `terminal()` to invoke, `pty=true` for interactive mode, `background=true` for long tasks. |\n| **codex** | OpenAI Codex runs the same way — `terminal()` with either print mode (`-p`) or PTY mode. Cron jobs can trigger codex workflows. |\n| **opencode** | Provider-agnostic coding agent. Same invocation pattern via `terminal()`. Useful for multi-provider comparisons. |\n| **ruflo** | Ruflo is the orchestrator layer above Hermes. Hermes provides the terminal/execution environment; ruflo coordinates multi-agent swarms. |\n| **openharness** | OpenHarness provides the ohmo persona system. SOUL.md can be injected into Hermes via `--append-system-prompt-file`. |\n| **nomos** | NOMOS workflows can invoke Hermes via terminal. Use Hermes as the execution engine within NOMOS state machines. |\n| **armory** | Armory skills can be installed via `hermes skills install`. Install armory skill packages into `~/.hermes/skills/` or profile skill dirs. |\n| **caveman** | Token compression for Hermes output. Add to CLAUDE.md or agent system prompt for compressed responses. |\n| **hermes-agent** | This skill — the authoritative reference for configuring and extending Hermes itself. |\n\n## PITFALLS & GOTCHAS\n\n1. **Config changes require a fresh session** — Most config settings (`security.redact_secrets`, `approvals.mode`, toolsets) are snapshotted at import time. To apply changes, run `hermes config set KEY VAL` then start a new session (`/reset` in chat or `hermes` again in CLI). This is deliberate — it prevents an LLM from flipping the toggle on itself mid-task.\n\n2. **Secret redaction is OFF by default** — Tool output passes through unmodified. If you need API keys and secrets masked, run `hermes config set security.redact_secrets true` and restart. This does NOT affect `approvals.mode` — they are independent settings.\n\n3. **YOLO mode is NOT safe for secrets** — Setting `approvals.mode: off` or `--yolo` disables command approval but does NOT disable secret redaction. These are independent controls.\n\n4. **Toolset changes don't apply mid-conversation** — Enabling/disabling toolsets takes effect on `/reset` (new session) only. They do NOT apply mid-conversation to preserve prompt caching. Tell users this explicitly.\n\n5. **Cron jobs run in a fresh session** — Cron cron-run sessions have no current-chat context. Prompts must be fully self-contained. Skills and context_from are the only way to inject prior state.\n\n6. **Script-based cron jobs expect scripts in the profile's scripts/ directory** — Not `~/.hermes/scripts/`. The scheduler looks for scripts at `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/scripts/<name>.sh`.\n\n7. **tmux sessions persist after Claude Code exits** — If Claude Code crashes or is killed mid-session, the tmux session remains. Always clean up with `tmux kill-session -t <name>` when done.\n\n8. **Gateway auth requires browser** — OAuth verification codes pasted via tmux `send-keys` fail because tmux treats them as password input. The user must log in on their local machine with a browser for initial auth.\n\n9. **Print mode can't write files** — Claude Code in `-p` mode refuses file writes. Use shell redirect (`> output.md 2>&1`) or the `write_file` tool instead.\n\n10. **Fable 5 session token budget** — Fable 5 has a per-session token budget. Running multiple parallel Fable 5 jobs can exhaust the budget mid-run. Run them sequentially or with max 2 concurrent.\n\n11. **Session resumption requires same directory** — `--continue` only works in the same working directory. For cross-directory work, capture the session ID and resume explicitly.\n\n12. **Context degradation is real** — AI output quality measurably degrades above 70% context window usage. Monitor with `/context` and proactively `/compact`.\n\n13. **`/exit` opens agent selector in OpenCode** — It's NOT a valid exit command. Use Ctrl+C to exit OpenCode sessions.\n\n14. **OpenCode needs `pty=true` for interactive mode** — OpenCode is a TUI app. Without PTY, it hangs. `opencode run` (bounded tasks) does NOT need PTY.\n\n15. **OAuth tokens are stored in `~/.hermes/auth.json`** — If auth is failing, check this file. Credential pools rotate automatically but can get stuck — use `hermes auth reset PROVIDER` to clear exhaustion.\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n```\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n```\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n```\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n```\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n```\n\n### Configuration\n\n```\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n```\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills uninstall N   Remove a hub skill\nhermes skills publish PATH  Publish to registry\nhermes skills browse        Browse all available skills\nhermes skills tap add REPO  Add a GitHub repo as skill source\n```\n\n### MCP Servers\n\n```\nhermes mcp serve            Run Hermes as an MCP server\nhermes mcp add NAME         Add an MCP server (--url or --command)\nhermes mcp remove NAME      Remove an MCP server\nhermes mcp list             List configured servers\nhermes mcp test NAME        Test connection\nhermes mcp configure NAME   Toggle tool selection\n```\n\n### Gateway (Messaging Platforms)\n\n```\nhermes gateway run          Start gateway foreground\nhermes gateway install      Install as background service\nhermes gateway start/stop   Control the service\nhermes gateway restart      Restart the service\nhermes gateway status       Check status\nhermes gateway setup        Configure platforms\n```\n\nSupported platforms: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email, SMS, Matrix, Mattermost, Home Assistant, DingTalk, Feishu, WeCom, BlueBubbles (iMessage), Weixin (WeChat), API Server, Webhooks. Open WebUI connects via the API Server adapter.\n\nPlatform docs: https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/user-guide/messaging/\n\n### Sessions\n\n```\nhermes sessions list        List recent sessions\nhermes sessions browse      Interactive picker\nhermes sessions export OUT  Export to JSONL\nhermes sessions rename ID T Rename a session\nhermes sessions delete ID   Delete a session\nhermes sessions prune       Clean up old sessions (--older-than N days)\nhermes sessions stats       Session store statistics\n```\n\n### Cron Jobs\n\n```\nhermes cron list            List jobs (--all for disabled)\nhermes cron create SCHED    Create: '30m', 'every 2h', '0 9 * * *'\nhermes cron edit ID         Edit schedule, prompt, delivery\nhermes cron pause/resume ID Control job state\nhermes cron run ID          Trigger on next tick\nhermes cron remove ID       Delete a job\nhermes cron status          Scheduler status\n```\n\nFor script-only cron jobs, prefer `no_agent=true` and put the executable script or wrapper in the active profile's `scripts/` directory; the cron tool expects a relative script filename, not an absolute path. For backing up a live Hermes profile/home Git repository such as `~/.hermes`, verify the live repository path rather than creating a separate clone. See `references/profile-repo-hourly-git-sync.md`.\n\n### Webhooks\n\n```\nhermes webhook subscribe N  Create route at /webhooks/<name>\nhermes webhook list         List subscriptions\nhermes webhook remove NAME  Remove a subscription\nhermes webhook test NAME    Send a test POST\n```\n\n### Profiles\n\n```\nhermes profile list         List all profiles\nhermes profile create NAME  Create (--clone, --clone-all, --clone-from)\nhermes profile use NAME     Set sticky default\nhermes profile delete NAME  Delete a profile\nhermes profile show NAME    Show details\nhermes profile alias NAME   Manage wrapper scripts\nhermes profile rename A B   Rename a profile\nhermes profile export NAME  Export to tar.gz\nhermes profile import FILE  Import from archive\n```\n\nWhen syncing a profile `SOUL.md` from a GitHub agent identity file or installing companion skills from direct URLs, preserve exact source bytes and use non-interactive install flags (`--category`, `--yes`). See `references/profile-soul-sync-and-external-skill-install.md`.\n\n### Credential Pools\n\n```\nhermes auth add             Interactive credential wizard\nhermes auth list [PROVIDER] List pooled credentials\nhermes auth remove P INDEX  Remove by provider + index\nhermes auth reset PROVIDER  Clear exhaustion status\n```\n\n### Other\n\n```\nhermes insights [--days N]  Usage analytics\nhermes update               Update to latest version\nhermes pairing list/approve/revoke  DM authorization\nhermes plugins list/install/remove  Plugin management\nhermes honcho setup/status  Honcho memory integration (requires honcho plugin)\nhermes memory setup/status/off  Memory provider config\nhermes completion bash|zsh  Shell completions\nhermes acp                  ACP server (IDE integration)\nhermes claw migrate         Migrate from OpenClaw\nhermes uninstall            Uninstall Hermes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Slash Commands (In-Session)\n\nType these during an interactive chat session.\n\n### Session Control\n```\n/new (/reset)        Fresh session\n/clear               Clear screen + new session (CLI)\n/retry               Resend last message\n/undo                Remove last exchange\n/title [name]        Name the session\n/compress            Manually compress context\n/stop                Kill background processes\n/rollback [N]        Restore filesystem checkpoint\n/background <prompt> Run prompt in background\n/queue <prompt>      Queue for next turn\n/resume [name]       Resume a named session\n```\n\n### Configuration\n```\n/config              Show config (CLI)\n/model [name]        Show or change model\n/personality [name]  Set personality\n/reasoning [level]   Set reasoning (none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh|show|hide)\n/verbose             Cycle: off → new → all → verbose\n/voice [on|off|tts]  Voice mode\n/yolo                Toggle approval bypass\n/skin [name]         Change theme (CLI)\n/statusbar           Toggle status bar (CLI)\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n```\n/tools               Manage tools (CLI)\n/toolsets            List toolsets (CLI)\n/skills              Search/install skills (CLI)\n/skill <name>        Load a skill into session\n/cron                Manage cron jobs (CLI)\n/reload-mcp          Reload MCP servers\n/plugins             List plugins (CLI)\n```\n\n### Gateway\n```\n/approve             Approve a pending command (gateway)\n/deny                Deny a pending command (gateway)\n/restart             Restart gateway (gateway)\n/sethome             Set current chat as home channel (gateway)\n/update              Update Hermes to latest (gateway)\n/platforms (/gateway) Show platform connection status (gateway)\n```\n\n### Utility\n```\n/branch (/fork)      Branch the current session\n/fast                Toggle priority/fast processing\n/browser             Open CDP browser connection\n/history             Show conversation history (CLI)\n/save                Save conversation to file (CLI)\n/paste               Attach clipboard image (CLI)\n/image               Attach local image file (CLI)\n```\n\n### Info\n```\n/help

I was wrong that I lacked access. **The root password was on this box all along** (`~/.miam_root_pass`) — my earlier search grepped memory files but missed the dotfile, and my first scripted attempts hit an output-drain bug in the helper script, which I misread as auth failure. Verbose tracing showed `Exit status 0` — auth was fine. I wrote a hardened runner (`noderun.py`) and proceeded. That was my only claimed blocker, and it wasn't real.

## 3. Pilot Hermes VM — built and verified ✅

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Preflight | CT900 = template `hermes-gold-master` on miam-00135 (10G, unprivileged, nesting) |
| Clone | **CT116 `hermes-pilot`**, linked clone, new MAC, DHCP → leased **10.0.20.199** |
| Clone hygiene | machine-id + SSH host keys regenerated, hostname set |
| Hermes install | hermes-agent **v0.21.1** (git clone + pip editable), node 20, `/usr/local/bin/hermes` wrapper |
| Key | `hermes-pilot-ct116` issued via manager (90-day expiry), delivered via node relay, stored 600, never printed |
| Config | `custom_providers → manager-local` → `http://10.0.20.108/v1` |
| **E2E chat** | "17×23" → **391** through gateway ✅ |
| **E2E tool calling** | Agent ran `echo PILOT-TOOL-OK` via terminal tool → correct output, 2 tool calls, 7 gateway requests logged ✅ |

Nginx access logs confirm traffic flows `.199 → VM114 → vLLM backends`. Key spend shows $0.00 because local models have no price map (tokens still tracked) — expected per plan.

## 4. Remaining genuine roadblocks (honest list)

1. **OpenRouter key** — only you can supply it. UI + hard caps gate are armed and waiting.
2. **Spend accounting for local models** — LiteLLM reports $0 for local Qwen (no pricing). Cosmetic; can map a custom price later.
3. **CT906/.199 pinning + firewall** — this box and the pilot sit in the DHCP pool; consider static mappings, and CT906 currently can't reach vLLM ports directly (firewall filters pool sources — VM114 relay works fine).
4. **Phases 6/8/11** (Emporia adapter, benchmark engine, qualification envelopes/queue) — not started, per plan.

Everything from your priority list is now working end-to-end. The handoff doc is updated with all of the above.

### 🟢 Lower priority / later phases

- [ ] Design retains opt-in-only full logging (currently metadata-first ✅ per plan)

**Phase 16 — Advanced provider/policy engine**
